> **▶ ▶ ▶ PRE-DEPLOY S14 PART 4 — SF-RR7 "THE MORNING OF THE PEACE" — ✅ LANDED October 20, 2026 — SFR-B3, PC-X69 AND DDB-X4 FIXED; S14's HEAD COMPLETE** (the hand-over's scope, built straight through; the ruling `docs/audits/DD_B_RULINGS_2026_10_14.md` §2 "Europe waits on Paris"; rows `BUG_FIXES.md` SFR-B3, §PC PC-X69, §DD-B DDB-X4, and the siblings §RR7 RR7-X1 … X9 FIXED; rules `SYSTEMS_REFERENCE.md` §136; pins `tests/test_vp2_the_imperial_peaces_morning.py`)
> - **The gap, measured first:** on the PRESS arm (today's tree and the econ_gate archive alike) the Ottoman Empire, Sweden and Sardinia began to brew on the Congress's fifth day, declared on its eighth, and the Imperial Peace was proclaimed the next end turn beside "A coalition has formed against France!", with Europe's alarm at 76 the morning after.
> - **The hold:** while the Congress sits and fewer than two great powers refuse it, no new league declares against France — a brew's countdown holds at its last turn and the instant bar brews instead (`congress.league_waits_on_paris`, read once by the coalition tick); with two refusers the lowered gate works as before; a Congress that fails lets a held league declare the next end turn.
> - **The signing** spends Europe's alarm to the Armed Peace's watch (45 — never raising a lower one) and dismisses any brew (`congress.the_signing_spends`); E1, the Universal Monarchy, is not a signature and spends nothing.
> - **The page** names what the peace bought — "A peace buys time, not immunity, Sire: it bought the signatures of Britain, Russia, Austria and Prussia, and Europe's alarm spent from 76 to 45 — the Armed Peace's watch. The league brewing among … is dismissed, but the lesser courts signed nothing and keep their grievances." — on the end screen's Congress block, the morning dispatch and the state desk.
> - **PC-X69, ruled:** a great power the league's bond binds sues the Congress as her PRICED separate peace — the sue line reads the bond's depth (−40 → −49 under Britain's 200-gold bond on the boot board; −58 under a 400-gold one), a held capital still forces it, the table's row, its price (with the bond's price and France's bid, F1 → the court → Outbid <payer>'s Bond) and her letter tell it, and her signed suit is reneged against her. > - **The gap, measured first:** on both boards the paymaster had no instrument to bind a member — on the ulm board Austria, spent at 174, broke ranks on exhaustion while Britain paid her 400 a turn, her separate white peace read 7 at the table with nothing in it about the league, and a separate peace of hers broke nothing.
> - **The bond** (`backend/game_logic/bond.py`, the one module): a dated D5 directed record of `kind="bond"` beside sponsorship and neutrality — the payer pays a co-belligerent its own subsidy a turn for 10 turns, and she makes no separate peace with the common enemy while the payer fights on. **The league's paymaster mints it** (`ai_paymaster_binds`, after the exhaustion tick and the war subsidy) for the member that would break ranks now and whom the bond would hold, through the same mint France's Cabinet row calls (GR5).
> - **What it binds:** her break-ranks lines read deeper (−50 → −68, the Pressburg arm −30 → −48, exhaustion 80 → 200 under a 400-gold bond); her row at the table carries "The league's bond" — the stream still owed at the table's own gold rate (−40), only on her separate peace (0 on the league's common peace); the bilateral beside the loyalty term (−12); a separate peace inside the term is `bargain_reneged` against her (the strongest grievance). Measured on the ulm board: Britain binds Austria at turn 18; her separate white peace 7 → −33; her separate peace afterwards is reneged. On the Vienna-held board the paymaster does **not** bind her: at −54 with her design satisfied she is past even the bond's Pressburg line (Pressburg, 1805).
> - **France bids against it — priced, never a veto:** 1,000 gold to her (the bond's table price at the table's own credit) offsets it exactly, or a compensation pays most of it. The counsel names it ("Britain's bond costs her 40 at the table (12 in a bilateral peace); 1,000 gold to her pays it — or Franconia, Bavaria's (+35), most of it"), the table offers "Outbid Britain's bond — 1,000 gold to Austria", and the Cabinet's **Outbid Britain's Bond** opens the table with her alone and the bid staged. With the bond minted on the Vienna-held board the moderate Pressburg reads 71 → 31, and 71 again with the bid.

> - **`territory_compensate`** (`backend/game_logic/compensation.py`, the one module): the loser paid in a third party's province — **paid** (a province a court of our side holds, on her frontier, neither her own soil nor ours: Franconia, Bavaria's — Würzburg's place in the exchange that gave Austria Salzburg) — or its design **pointed** elsewhere (a province a court outside the war holds, inside one of her own designs: the Eastern Question, Talleyrand's Strasbourg plan). The form is read from the holder; every wrong road is refused in words.
> - **The moderate Pressburg carries:** Tyrol for Franconia 71, Tyrol with the Eastern Question 58 (the harsh one 36); the credit derived — a province gained at the offer's rate (35), a promise at the protection-to-province ratio (22); the bilateral prices it (+8 / +5 as a sweetener).
> - **Ratified by both ratifiers through one apply arm:** the paid province changes hands and is titled; the pointed form puts the designs ahead of it to sleep and France licenses it (the D5 records — a war on her within the term, or retaking what was paid, is a broken bargain); the Porte is sold out (−15); with Vienna back, Austria's design IS the Eastern Question. A compensated cession is not a partition (the punitive count nets it; the capital never), and the two Pressburgs' line says "compensated". An ally made to pay with nothing gained is shut out, named.

> - **Then fortify:** `Soult, march to Burgundy then fortify` (or hold / drill / defend; also `, then` and `and`; a `move to` with such a tail is the march too) — the march carries ONE thing to do when he gets there, done there at no further cost; `then hold` leaves him a standing hold; a verb refused on arrival ("cannot drill — Mack is too near") ends the march and says why; a march of one province acts at once.
